(106)

The theory in the left figure has no superpotential, and it is dual to a theory on the right with a superpotential
\mathcal{W}=\rm qM\tilde{q}
. In what follows we consider analogous processes of adding superpotentials for theories encoded in plumbing graphs with matter.

4.2
Gauging SQED-XYZ duality

Analogously to gauging of mirror triality, we now gauge the flavor symmetry of SQED-XYZ duality, thereby introducing additional gauge nodes in diagrams that encode two sides of this duality. We also find that the presence of superpotential implies certain non-trivial relations between mixed
Chern-Simons
levels assigned to a triangle in a graph representing one side of this duality.

To analyze the process of gauging, we consider the sphere partition functions of SQED and XYZ model. As mentioned in (
104
), these partition functions are equal. For later convenience, we rewrite this equality and move one term to the left

\displaystyle s_{b}\left(-\frac{iQ}{2}+2y\right)\int dx\,e^{-2\pi i\,zx}s_{b}\left(\frac{iQ}{2}\pm x-y\right)=s_{b}(y\pm z),

(107)

where
s_{b}(x\pm y):=s_{b}(x+y)s_{b}(x-y)
. This relation can be also interpreted as equality of sphere partition functions for the mirror pair (
92
). In this case, the superpotential of the theory
U(1)_{0}+1\mathbf{F}+1\mathbf{AF}+1\bf{Adj}
yields non-trivial relations between its three chiral multiplets. Note that the flavor charge for the adjoint chiral multiplet is 2, and we denote flavor symmetries associated with mass parameters
y
and
z
by
U(1)_{y}\times U(1)_{z}

4
4

4

Note that
U(1)_{z}
is the topological symmetry on SQED side.
.

One can draw plumbing graphs for this mirror pair, however generic Kirby moves do not lead to interesting theories. Therefore we gauge flavor symmetries, which leads to dual theories represented by the following plumbing graphs

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[]{Figures/SQEDXYZgauge}\end{split}

(108)

Gauging the flavor symmetry
U(1)_{y}\times U(1)_{z}
introduces two additional gauge nodes with variables
y
and
z
respectively. Note that one could move the adjoint matter to the right graph, which would represent gauging of the SQED-XYZ mirror pair; however, this would lead to the same results that we find for the above graph pair (
108
), so we only discuss this pair in what follows. Furthermore, note that two matter nodes in the right graph have different charges
(+1,-1)
and
(+1,+1)
, which is important, since if they would have the same or opposite charges, then mixed
Chern-Simons
levels could not be generated upon
ST
transformations.

The gauging of graphs in (
108
) is still not complete, as we have not turned on mixed
Chern-Simons
levels for new gauge nodes
U(1)_{z}
and
U(1)_{y}
. After doing so, we get the gauged duality represented by the following graphs

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/mirrorpairplumb}\end{split}

(109)

where
a
,
b
and
c
are
Chern-Simons
levels for
U(1)_{y}
and
U(1)_{z}
gauge groups respectively, which should be integer or half-integer. Since we are gauging global symmetries for dual theories, the introduced
Chern-Simons
levels and FI parameters on both sides of the duality should be the same.
However, since our purpose is to perform Kirby moves and
ST
transformations on the graphs in (
109
) to see how superpotentials are encoded in these graphs, we do not discuss the values for
a
,
b
and
c
at this moment, as generic Kirby moves change
Chern-Simons
levels significantly.

The full gauging of flavor symmetries adds the following integral to sphere partition functions on both sides of the duality

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{gauge}[y,z]:=\int dy\int dz\,e^{-\pi i(ay^{2}+bz^{2}+2c\,yz)}e^{2\pi i\,(\xi_{y}y+\xi_{z}z)},

(110)

so that the sphere partition functions for the gauged mirror pair are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boxed{\int\textbf{gauge}[y,z]\int dx\,e^{-2\pi i\,zx}s_{b}\left(-\frac{iQ}{2}+2y\right)s_{b}\left(\frac{iQ}{2}\pm x-y\right)=\int\textbf{gauge}[y,z]~s_{b}(y\pm z)}\\
\end{split}

(111)

where again
s_{b}(x\pm y):=s_{b}(x+y)s_{b}(x-y)
. We also refer to this relation as a 2-3 move, as the numbers of chiral multiplets on both sides are 3 and 2 respectively. After this gauging, all chiral multiplets become massless, as all free mass parameters are gauged.

More generally, we could also turn on non-trivial charges for
y
and
z
, by replacing
y\rightarrow q_{1}y
and
z\rightarrow q_{2}z
, and then gauging the corresponding flavor symmetries
U(1)_{y}\times U(1)_{z}
. However, the computation shows that this does not influence the final results that we will discuss in the following sections, because after gauging, we will integrate out undecorated gauge nodes
U(1)_{y}
and
U(1)_{z}
, which does not affect their charges. Therefore, in the following we only discuss Kirby moves and
ST
-moves for the pair of theories in (
109
).

In what follows we consider a pair of dual complicated theories represented by the graphs in (
109
) and transform them further, using
ST
-moves and Kirby moves, in particular transforming bifundamental matter fields into fundamental ones. Ultimately we obtain dualities between theories represented by balanced graphs (whose number of gauge nodes is the same as the number of matter nodes). We find that (
109
) can be transformed into operations that we call unlinking and linking, in analogy with operations on quivers in knots-quivers correspondence. In addition, we find a few other operations that we call exotic ones. As we will see, existence of superpotentials in these theories imposes certain constraints on mixed
Chern-Simons
levels.

Unlinking

To start with, we consider the graph on the right hand side in (
109
). We apply
ST
-moves to two bifundamental chiral multiplets and then integrate out pure gauge nodes denoted by orange circles:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/acbtorks}\end{split}

(112)

In this way we obtain a two-node graph with effective
Chern-Simons
levels
r
,
s
, and
k
, which are related to
a
,
b
, and
c
as follows:

\displaystyle r=\frac{ab-(1+c)^{2}}{1+a+b+ab-c^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=\frac{ab-(1-c)^{2}}{1+a+b+ab-c^{2}}\,,\quad

\displaystyle k

\displaystyle=\frac{a-b}{1+a+b+ab-c^{2}}\,,

(113)

\displaystyle a=\frac{-1-2k-k^{2}+rs}{-1+k^{2}+r+s-rs}\,,\quad

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=\frac{-1+2k-k^{2}+rs}{-1+k^{2}+r+s-rs}\,,\quad

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=\frac{r-s}{-1+k^{2}+r+s-rs}\,.

(114)

For future convenience, we also introduce the notation
(i,j, …)
to denote implementing
ST
-moves
(ST)^{\textbf{i}},(ST)^{\textbf{j}},\ldots
respectively on the first, second, etc. matter node of a given balanced graph. For example, in the last graph in (
112
) we do not apply further
ST
-moves on any of the two matter nodes, so we denote this graph by
(0,0)
. The assignment of the above effective
Chern-Simons
levels to this graph can be represented by

\displaystyle\textbf{ (0,0)}~~\rightarrow~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}\left(\hbox to13.78pt{\vbox to2.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.2pt\lower 4.49046pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 7.87 M 1.38 7.87 C 1.38 8.64 0.76 9.26 0 9.26 C -0.76 9.26 -1.38 8.64 -1.38 7.87 C -1.38 7.11 -0.76 6.49 0 6.49 C 0.76 6.49 1.38 7.11 1.38 7.87 Z M 0 7.87}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 15.75 7.87 M 17.13 7.87 C 17.13 8.64 16.51 9.26 15.75 9.26 C 14.98 9.26 14.36 8.64 14.36 7.87 C 14.36 7.11 14.98 6.49 15.75 6.49 C 16.51 6.49 17.13 7.11 17.13 7.87 Z M 15.75 7.87}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 15.75 7.87 L 0 7.87}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\right)=\begin{bmatrix}r&k\\
k&s\\
\end{bmatrix}

(115)

We now transform the left hand side of (
109
) into a balanced graph. Such a graph can be obtain in various ways and its mixed
Chern-Simons
levels are rational functions of
a,b
and
c
, or equivalently rational functions of
k,r
and
s
in (
113
). In order to avoid partity anomaly
Aharony:1997aa
we look for a balanced graph whose mixed
Chern-Simons
levels are integer, if
k,r
and
s
are integer – in other words, such mixed
Chern-Simons
levels should be polynomials in
k,r
and
s
. We find that such a graph can be obtain in the following process:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/unlinkCS}\end{split}

(116)

where we use a yellow circle to track positions of two initially bifundamental chiral multiplets. The adjoint chiral multiplet in the first graph is turned into a fundamental chiral multiplet in the second step and then moved to the top of triangle graph in the third step. The orange circles mark the undecorated gauge nodes that we integrate out. Note that already the third graph in (
116
) is balanced, however its mixed
Chern-Simons
levels are fractional when expressed in terms of (integer)
k,r
, and
s
. Therefore, we apply further
ST
-moves
\bf(1,1,0)
on this graph, in order to obtain mixed
Chern-Simons
levels which are polynomials in
k,r
, and
s

\displaystyle\textbf{(1,1,0)}~~\rightarrow~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}(\hbox to11.1pt{\vbox to8.83pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.55238pt\lower-7.82874pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.11 0 C 1.11 0.61 0.61 1.11 0 1.11 C -0.61 1.11 -1.11 0.61 -1.11 0 C -1.11 -0.61 -0.61 -1.11 0 -1.11 C 0.61 -1.11 1.11 -0.61 1.11 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 6.3 -9.45 M 7.41 -9.45 C 7.41 -8.84 6.91 -8.34 6.3 -8.34 C 5.69 -8.34 5.19 -8.84 5.19 -9.45 C 5.19 -10.06 5.69 -10.56 6.3 -10.56 C 6.91 -10.56 7.41 -10.06 7.41 -9.45 Z M 6.3 -9.45}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -6.3 -9.45 M -5.19 -9.45 C -5.19 -8.84 -5.69 -8.34 -6.3 -8.34 C -6.91 -8.34 -7.41 -8.84 -7.41 -9.45 C -7.41 -10.06 -6.91 -10.56 -6.3 -10.56 C -5.69 -10.56 -5.19 -10.06 -5.19 -9.45 Z M -6.3 -9.45}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 6.3 -9.45 L -6.3 -9.45 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}})=\begin{bmatrix}s&k-1&s+k-1\\
k-1&r&r+k-1\\
s+k-1&r+k-1&r+s+2k-1\end{bmatrix}

(117)

To sum up, we have transformed the gauged SQED-XYZ duality (
109
) into the following duality, which we refer to as the 2-3 move. In analogy with the operation in knots-quiver correspondence
Ekholm:2019lmb
we also call it unlinking, as the linking number
k
for the two original nodes decreases by one

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/unlinkrelations}\end{split}

(118)

We summarize the relation between associated mixed
Chern-Simons
levels as follows

\displaystyle k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}\left(-\right)~~\xrightarrow{\text{unlinking}}~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}(\bigtriangleup)\,.

(119)

A manifestation of the superpotantial of the XYZ model are the relations between mixed
Chern-Simons
levels in the triangle graph in (
118
), i.e. the fact that all these
Chern-Simons
levels are determined by three parameters
k,r
and
s
.
Note that for
r=s=0
and
k=1
, this dual pair reduces to the original 2-3 move for SQED-XYZ duality, whose graphs are shown in (
288
).

Moreover, FI parameters on both sides of the duality (
118
) are also related. Denoting FI parameters for the gauge nodes
U(1)_{r}
and
U(1)_{s}
respectively by
x_{r}=e^{\xi_{r}}
and
x_{s}=e^{\xi_{s}}
, we find that the FI parameter for the new gauge node
U(1)_{r+s+2k-1}
takes form
q^{*}x_{r}x_{s}
, i.e. under the duality these parameters are transformed as follows

\displaystyle(x_{r}\,,x_{s})~\longrightarrow~(x_{r}\,,x_{s}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{*}x_{r}x_{s})

(120)

where
q^{*}
can be found by comparing the sphere partition functions. Therefore, in this sense three decorated gauge nodes of the triangle are also not independent.

Note that the integral triangle graph may not be unique for specific values
r
,
s
and
k
, because we can perform
ST
-moves on the unlinking triangle of (
118
). However, these additional triangles are easy to find by scanning all graphs, as one can perform
ST
-moves at most three times on each matter node, and hence there are in total
3^{\text{rank of flavors}}=3^{3}=27
graphs for a triangle. For example, the
Chern-Simons
levels of the third graph in (
116
) can be integers for some particular values of
r
,
k
and
s
. Moreover, the permutation group of gauge nodes is a subset of the
ST
-move group
S_{3}\subset 3^{\text{rank of flavors}}
. The relations between FI parameters can always be tracked by the evolution of sphere partition functions undergoing
ST
-moves.

Linking

Another interesting form of duality, which can be obtained using some specific
ST
-moves, we call linking, also in analogy with knots-quivers correspondence
Ekholm:2019lmb
. Let us apply
ST
-moves
({\mathbf{1}},{\mathbf{1}})
on the two-node graph in (
112
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[]{Figures/rskSTST}\end{split}

(121)

After integrating out pure gauge nodes in orange circles, we denote mixed
Chern-Simons
levels for the resulting two-node graph by

\displaystyle\textbf{ (1,1)}~~\rightarrow~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}\left(-\right)=\begin{bmatrix}r^{\prime}&k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime}&s^{\prime}\\
\end{bmatrix}

(122)

and we find the following relations

\displaystyle r^{\prime}=\frac{rs-k^{2}-r}{s-1}\,,\quad s^{\prime}=\frac{-1}{s-1}\,,\quad k^{\prime}=\frac{k}{s-1}.

(123)

Obviously, for generic
(r,s,k)
, one may not get integral
(r^{\prime},s^{\prime},k^{\prime})
.

In this case, we find that the dual three-node graphs should undergo a different process in comparison with the unlinking case (
116
), in order to end up with a triangle with integral
Chern-Simons
levels:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/linkCS}\end{split}

(124)

The first three graphs of (
116
) are the same as that of (
124
), while the next two operations are different. The last triangle of (
124
) is obtained by performing
(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{1}})

ST
-move on matter nodes and integrating out an undecorated gauge node. The mixed
Chern-Simons
levels for the last triangle are

\displaystyle\textbf{ (0,0,1)}~~\rightarrow~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}(\bigtriangleup)=\begin{bmatrix}r^{\prime}&k^{\prime}+1&r^{\prime}+k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime}+1&s^{\prime}&s^{\prime}+k^{\prime}\\
r^{\prime}+k^{\prime}&s^{\prime}+k^{\prime}&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}+2k^{\prime}\end{bmatrix}

(125)

Assuming that
(r^{\prime},s^{\prime},k^{\prime})
are integral, this triangle also has integral
Chern-Simons
levels too. Moreover, for specific values of
(r^{\prime},s^{\prime},k^{\prime})
, there may be other triangles with integral
Chern-Simons
levels, which can be easily identified by scanning all graphs in the orbit of
ST
-moves on the special triangle (
125
).

To summarize, we have found another duality, which we refer to as linking:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/linkrelation}\end{split}\,.

(126)

As in the linking operation discussed in
Ekholm:2019lmb
, in our case the linking number
k^{\prime}
of the two-node graph also increases by one. The associated
Chern-Simons
levels are related by

\displaystyle k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}\left(-\right)~~\xrightarrow{\text{linking}}~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}({\bf{\bigtriangleup}}),

(127)

and the relation between FI parameters takes analogous form as in the unlinking case

\displaystyle(x_{r^{\prime}}\,,x_{s^{\prime}})~\longrightarrow~(x_{r^{\prime}}\,,x_{s^{\prime}}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{**}x_{r^{\prime}}x_{s^{\prime}}),

(128)

for some particular parameter
\mathfrak{q}^{**}
.

Exotic cases

Apart from linking and unlinking, we find also two other interesting operations that we call exotic, which transform a two-node graph into a triangle with integral mixed
Chern-Simons
levels. Let us perform
ST
-move
(\mathbf{0},{\mathbf{1}})
only on one node of the two-node graph:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[]{Figures/rskSTodd}\end{split}

(129)

The resulting two-node graph has mixed
Chern-Simons
levels

\displaystyle\textbf{ (0,1)}~~\rightarrow~~k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}\left(-\right)=\begin{bmatrix}r^{\prime}&k^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime}&s^{\prime}\\
\end{bmatrix}

(130)

such that

\displaystyle r^{\prime}=\frac{rs-k^{2}}{s}\,,\quad k^{\prime}=\frac{-k}{s}\,,\quad s^{\prime}=\frac{s-1}{s}.

(131)

Scanning dual triangle graphs, we find that there are two triangle graphs with integral
Chern-Simons
levels that are obtained by
ST
-moves
(2,0,1)
and
(2,1,0)
respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\textbf{(2,0,1)}\rightarrow\begin{bmatrix}r^{\prime}&k^{\prime}-r^{\prime}&k^{\prime}-r^{\prime}+1\\
k^{\prime}-r^{\prime}&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1\\
k^{\prime}-r^{\prime}+1&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1\\
\end{bmatrix}\\
&\textbf{(2,1,0)}\rightarrow\begin{bmatrix}s^{\prime}&k^{\prime}-s^{\prime}+1&k^{\prime}-s^{\prime}\\
k^{\prime}-s^{\prime}+1&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1\\
k^{\prime}-s^{\prime}&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}-1&r^{\prime}+s^{\prime}-2k^{\prime}\\
\end{bmatrix}\end{split}

(132)

The exotic operations that transform the rightmost graph in (
129
) into the graph captured by the first or the second matrix in (
132
) we denote respectively
\textbf{E}_{r^{\prime}}
and
\textbf{E}_{s^{\prime}}
. These operations preserve only one of the original
Chern-Simons
levels, respectively
r^{\prime}
or
s^{\prime}
, associated with
U(1)_{r^{\prime}}
or
U(1)_{s^{\prime}}
gauge group. Note that these two operations are related by an
ST
-move, as
\textbf{(2,0,1)}+\textbf{(0,1,2)}=\textbf{(2,1,0)}
. Moreover, for some particular values of
(r,k,s)
there exist other triangles with integral
Chern-Simons
levels, which can be obtained by further
ST
-moves on these two exotic triangles. To find such integral triangle graphs one can just scan theories in the orbit of
ST
-moves. From the form of sphere partition functions, we also find relations between FI parameters:

\displaystyle\textbf{E}_{r^{\prime}}:~~(x_{r^{\prime}}\,,x_{s^{\prime}})~\longrightarrow~\Big(x_{r^{\prime}}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{*}\frac{x_{s^{\prime}}}{x_{r^{\prime}}}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{*}\frac{x_{s^{\prime}}}{x_{r^{\prime}}}\,\Big)\,,

(133)

\displaystyle\textbf{E}_{s^{\prime}}:~~(x_{r^{\prime}}\,,x_{s^{\prime}})~\longrightarrow~\Big(x_{s^{\prime}}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{*}\frac{x_{r^{\prime}}}{x_{s^{\prime}}}\,,\mathfrak{q}^{*}\frac{x_{r^{\prime}}}{x_{s^{\prime}}}\,\Big)\,.

(134)

Relations between triangle graphs

To sum up, we have found a few theories dual to a two-node graph, which are represented by triangle graphs with integral mixed
Chern-Simons
levels. We refer to these dualities respectively as unlinking, linking, and exotic, and in what follows we denote the resulting triangle graphs by
A
,
B
and
E
:

(135)

As mentioned above,
\textbf{E}_{r}
and
\textbf{E}_{s}
are related by an
ST
-move, however other theories are not in the same orbit of
ST
-moves. The relations between all these triangle graphs can be represented by a commutative diagram:

(136)

where
\mathcal{W}
is the superpotential that is added during the gauged 2-3 move, so that the theories
A
,
B
and
E
are be related by the process
\mathcal{W}\circ ST^{*}\circ\mathcal{W}^{-1}
.

Let us present the structure of operations in (
135
) in more detail. Note that for simplicity, when we mention
ST
-moves in what follows, we implicitly assume that Kirby moves that remove pure gauge nodes have been also performed. First, the orbit of
ST
-moves for the two-node graph takes form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/orbitst}\end{split}

(137)

where the
(\textbf{m},\textbf{n})
denotes the
ST
-moves
((ST)^{m},(ST)^{n})
that act on the two-node graph. Each element of this orbit gives rise to a triangle graph with integral
Chern-Simons
levels, which encodes superpotential added during the 2-3 move. Separating the above graph into layers, we present the resulting dual triangle graphs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/STWrelation}\end{split}

(138)

The indices
({\textbf{m},\textbf{n},\textbf{l}})
of
A
,
B
, and
E
denote
ST
-moves
((ST)^{m},(ST)^{n},(ST)^{l})
that act on the matter nodes of three-node graphs; see (
116
) and (
124
) for examples.

Coupling external nodes

The gauged SQED-XYZ dual pair (
109
) is a local duality and it can be coupled to other nodes. Let us discuss such a coupling to one extra external node; adding more external nodes is a trivial extension. To begin with, adding one decorated node
U(1)_{c_{i}}+1\mathbf{F}
extends the dual pair (
109
) to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/mirrorpairXYZ1}\end{split}

(139)

This dual pair can be transformed into the following dual pair using
ST
-moves and Kirby moves, where we use colorful lines to mark different connections:

(140)

If an external node is not coupled to the two gauge nodes (marked in red) involved in 2-3 move, then it will also not connect to the triangle graph (marked in red) encoding the superpotential. Note that this gauged 2-3 move changes the couplings with the introduced gauge node in the dual superpotential triangle, however it does not change the couplings between the external node and the original two gauge nodes. More explicitly, for the unlinking we find

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|cc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k\\
q_{i}&k&s\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}~~\xrightarrow{\text{unlinking}}~~\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}+q_{i}}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k-1&{r+k-1}\\
q_{i}&k-1&s&{s+k-1}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}+q_{i}}&{r+k-1}&{s+k-1}&{r+s+2k-1}\end{array}\end{bmatrix}\,.

(141)

Notice that there is a new line connecting the external node
U(1)_{c_{i}}
and the introduced node. The corresponding
Chern-Simons
level takes value
p_{i}+q_{i}
and we denote it in blue. Similarly, linking leads to

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|cc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k\\
q_{i}&k&s\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}~~\xrightarrow{\text{linking}}~~\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}+q_{i}}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k+1&{r+k}\\
q_{i}&k+1&s&{s+k}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}+q_{i}}&{r+k}&{s+k}&{r+s+2k}\end{array}\end{bmatrix}\,.

(142)

For exotic triangles we have

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|cc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k\\
q_{i}&k&s\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}~~\xrightarrow{\text{exotic-}r}~~\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}g_{i}&p_{i}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q_{i}-p_{i}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q_{i}-p_{i}}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k-r&{k-r+1}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q_{i}-p_{i}}&k-r&r+s-2k&{r+s-2k-1}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}q_{i}-p_{i}}&{k-r+1}&{r+s-2k-1}&{r+s-2k-1}\end{array}\end{bmatrix}

(143)

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|cc}g_{i}&p_{i}&q_{i}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&r&k\\
q_{i}&k&s\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}~~\xrightarrow{\text{exotic-}s}~~\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}g_{i}&p_{i}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}-q_{i}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}-q_{i}}\\
\hline\cr p_{i}&s&k-s&{k-s+1}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}-q_{i}}&k-s&r+s-2k&{r+s-2k-1}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p_{i}-q_{i}}&{k-s+1}&{r+s-2k-1}&{r+s-2k-1}\end{array}\end{bmatrix}\,.

(144)

We emphasize that
ST
-moves (and Kirby moves) performed in the analysis of linking, unlinking and exotic dual graphs, should not affect the external node
U(1)_{c_{i}}
.

Importantly, the operations of linking and unlinking that involve extra nodes, are also consistent with general linking and unlinking operations in knots-quivers correspondence.

A case study:
(\textbf{2},\textbf{2})

ST
-move

As a special case we discuss
(\textbf{2},\textbf{2})
-operation. One reason to focus on this example is that in the following section we come accross a similar expression (
4.3
) (which however has a different meaning). The
(\textbf{2},\textbf{2})

ST
-move leads to the following graph:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/acbspecial22}\end{split}

(145)

Note that in this graph gauge nodes
U(1)_{a}
and
U(1)_{b}
cannot be exchanged as the charges for two bifundamental matter nodes are different, as shown in (
109
). The dual triangle graphs also have this property.
The integral triangle graphs are obtained by
\bf(0,1,2)
and
\bf(1,0,2)
operations, so that only the top node in the left graph in (
145
) is transformed, while the bottom one is not:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/specialtriangle1}\end{split}

(146)

Effective mixed
Chern-Simons
levels for these graphs are

\displaystyle k_{ij}^{\text{eff}}=\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{ccc}b+1&b&\frac{c-b-1}{2}\\
b&b&\frac{c-b+1}{2}\\
\frac{c-b-1}{2}&\frac{c-b+1}{2}&\frac{2+a+b-2c}{4}\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}\,,\quad\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{ccc}b&b&\frac{-c-b+1}{2}\\
b&b+1&\frac{-c-b-1}{2}\\
\frac{-c-b+1}{2}&\frac{-c-b-1}{2}&\frac{2+a+b+2c}{4}\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}

(147)

These two matrices only differ by the sign of
c
, and they are equal for
c=0
. In fact, these two triangle graphs are the exotic ones
\textbf{E}_{r}
and
\textbf{E}_{s}
that we discussed before, see (
138
). To see this explicitly, we replace
(a,b,c)
parameters by
(r,k,s)
defined by

\displaystyle a=r+s+2k-1\,,~~b=r+s-2k-1\,,~~c=\pm(r-s)\,.

(148)

This redefinition results in the follows
Chern-Simons
levels of the left graph in (
145
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/acbspecial22reverse}\end{split}

(149)

and also transforms triangles in (
146
) into exotic triangles that we discussed before.

Moreover, in the presence of an external gauge node
U(1)_{c_{i}}
, the dual pair (
139
)
leads to (
140
), and the associated
Chern-Simons
levels are

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|cc}c_{i}&\frac{b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}&\frac{-b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}\\
\hline\cr\frac{b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}&\frac{2+a+b-2c}{4}&\frac{a-b}{4}\\
\frac{-b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}&\frac{a-b}{4}&\frac{2+a+b+2c}{4}\\
\end{array}\end{bmatrix}~~\longleftrightarrow~~\begin{bmatrix}\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}c_{i}&\mp b_{i}&\mp b_{i}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{\pm b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}}\\
\hline\cr\mp b_{i}&b+1&b&\frac{\pm c-b-1}{2}\\
\mp b_{i}&b&b&{\frac{c-b+1}{2}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\frac{\pm b_{i}-a_{i}}{2}}&\frac{\pm c-b-1}{2}&{\frac{\pm c-b+1}{2}}&{\frac{2+a+b\mp 2c}{4}}\end{array}\end{bmatrix}

(150)

Note that only when the parameters
a,b,c,a_{i},b_{i}
are chosen properly, the dual pairs have integer
Chern-Simons
levels.

4.3
Partial gauging

In the previous section we fully gauged the flavor symmetry group
U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}
. In this section, we consider gauging of only its one subgroup
U(1)_{1}\subset U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}
. In this case we also find that the presence of superpotential implies non-trivial conditions on mixed
Chern-Simons
levels associated to dual triangle graphs. Note that partial gauging of
U(1)_{1}
can be also viewed as ungauging the
U(1)_{2}
of the fully gauged
U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}
.

Gauging
U(1)_{z}

In comparison with the full gauging (
110
), let us consider first only gauging of the flavor symmetry for
z
:

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{gauge}(z):=\int dz\,e^{-\pi i\,b\,z^{2}+2\pi i\,\xi_{z}z}\,.

(151)

Here the variable
y
is viewed as a mass parameter
\langle y\rangle=m
. The associated sphere partition functions are given by adding this integral on both sides of (
107
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boxed{s_{b}\left(-\frac{iQ}{2}+2m\right)\int\textbf{gauge}(z)\int dx\,e^{-2\pi i\,zx}s_{b}\left(\frac{iQ}{2}\pm x-m\right)=\int\textbf{gauge}(z)~s_{b}(m\pm z)}\\
\end{split}

(152)

The plumbing graphs resulting from the partial gauging are similar to the dual pair (
109
), for which we only need to delete the gauge node for
y
and lines connecting to it. In this case the adjoint chiral multiplet becomes a free chiral multiplet and decouples, so that the plumbing graph pair can be viewed as the operation of moving a pair of
1\mathbf{F}
and
1\mathbf{AF}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/higgsing}\end{split}

(153)

where for simplicity we do not draw the decoupled free chiral multiplet in the left graph.

The
ST
-moves of the graph on the right hand side (RHS) of (
153
) give rise to a chain (orbit) of theories with effective
Chern-Simons
levels:

\displaystyle\text{RHS:}~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b&1\\
1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b+1&-1\\
-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{b}{b+1}&\frac{1}{b+1}\\
\frac{1}{b+1}&\frac{b}{b+1}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~~~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{b-1}{b}&-\frac{1}{b}\\
-\frac{1}{b}&-\frac{1}{b}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~~~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{1-b}&\frac{1}{b-1}\\
\frac{1}{b-1}&\frac{1}{1-b}\\
\end{array}\right]

Integrating out the undecorated gauge node
U(1)_{b}
of the graph on the left hand side (LHS) of (
153
) and then performing
ST
-moves give rise to a chain of theories with effective
Chern-Simons
levels:

\displaystyle\text{LHS:}~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b+1&b\\
b&b\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{1-b}&\frac{b}{b-1}\\
\frac{b}{b-1}&\frac{1}{1-b}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{b}{b+1}&-\frac{b}{b+1}\\
-\frac{b}{b+1}&\frac{b}{b+1}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-\frac{1}{b}&1\\
1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{b-1}{b}&-1\\
-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]

Since (
153
) is a dual pair, we obtain interesting dualities (which we also call 2-2 moves) by picking up integral matrices from the two chains above:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS }\quad&\longleftrightarrow\quad\text{LHS}:\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b&1\\
1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b+1&-1\\
-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\quad&\longleftrightarrow\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}b+1&b\\
b&b\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(174)

In principle, the LHS should be encoded in triangle graphs, whose missing node is a free chiral multiplet contributing
s_{b}\left(-iQ/2+2m\right)
, which does not couple to other gauge nodes, so that we can ignore it and the three-node graph reduces to a two-node graph (the graph for decoupled free node is shown in (
278
)). Up to this extra node, one can check that the matrices on RHS can be obtained from those on LHS by operations of unlinking (
118
) and linking (
126
) (
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
), while matrices on LHS can be obtained from those on RHS by exotic dualities (
132
). Therefore, these operations form an algebra:

\displaystyle~~\textbf{E}_{r}=\textbf{A}^{-1}\,,~~\textbf{E}_{s}=\textbf{B}^{-1}\,,~~~~\textbf{E}_{r}=ST\circ\textbf{E}_{s}\,,~~\textbf{B}=ST\circ\textbf{A}\,,

(175)

where
ST
-moves relate
A
,
B
and
\textbf{E}_{r,s}
, and hence they are equivalent
\textbf{A}\sim\textbf{B}\sim\textbf{E}_{r,s}
. This feature does not appear when we fully gauge the flavor symmetry. This also implies that the superpotential does not exist for this 2-2 move in (
153
); this is also confirmed by the equivalence between theories given by (
153
) and (
98
), which do not carry superpotential. Note that this argument may only be correct for abelian theories, as the adjoint matter does not decouple for non-abelian theories.

In addition to the above dual pairs, we can identify also other dual pairs, if we choose other parametrizations:

\displaystyle r=\frac{b}{1+b}\,,~~-\frac{1}{b}\,,~~\frac{1}{1-b}\,.

(176)

In this case the other three matrices in (
4.3
) give rise to further dual pairs, respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS }~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\text{LHS}:\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&1-r\\
1-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&-r\\
-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r+1&r\\
r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r+1&-1\\
-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&1\\
1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&-r\\
-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&1-r\\
1-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(177)

Ignoring the decoupled matter node, the dual pairs in (
177
), from RHS to LHS, are
\textbf{A},\textbf{E}_{r,s}
and
B
respectively. Note that the first and the third line in (
177
) are the same, which implies that in this case

\textbf{B}=\textbf{A}^{-1}
. On the other hand, the second line is the same as (
174
), which implies
\textbf{E}_{r}=\textbf{A}^{-1},\textbf{E}_{s}=\textbf{B},\textbf{E}_{r}=ST\circ\textbf{E}_{s}
. We emphasize that (
177
) includes all possible dual pairs in this partial gauging.

Gauging
U(1)_{y}

Analogously we can consider partial gauging for
U(1)_{y}
, keeping
U(1)_{z}
as a flavor symmetry, so that the parameter
z
is viewed as a mass parameter,
\langle z\rangle=\tilde{m}
. This operation is represented by the integral

\displaystyle\int\mathbf{gauge}(y):=\int dy\,e^{-\pi ia\,y^{2}+2\pi i\,\xi_{y}y}

(178)

on both sides of (
107
), so that the equivalence of sphere partition functions takes form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\boxed{\int\textbf{gauge}(y)\int dx\,e^{-2\pi i\,\tilde{m}x}s_{b}\left(-\frac{iQ}{2}+2y\right)s_{b}\left(\frac{iQ}{2}\pm x-y\right)=\int\textbf{gauge}(y)~s_{b}(y\pm\tilde{m})}\\
\end{split}

(179)

The corresponding plumbing graphs can be obtained by deleting the gauge node
U(1)_{z}
and all lines connecting to this node:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/higgsing2}\end{split}

(180)

Interestingly, in this partial gauging the adjoint matter does not decouple, and hence it is still a 2-3 move. The two-node graph is the same as the two-node graph in (
153
), so we copy effective
Chern-Simons
levels for the orbit of
ST
-moves

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{RHS:}~~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}a+1&1\\
1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}a&-1\\
-1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{a}{a+1}&-\frac{1}{a+1}\\
-\frac{1}{a+1}&\frac{a}{a+1}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{a-1}{a}&\frac{1}{a}\\
\frac{1}{a}&-\frac{1}{a}\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{1-a}&\frac{1}{1-a}\\
\frac{1}{1-a}&\frac{1}{1-a}\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(181)

The left graph (three-node graph) in (
180
) leads to an orbit of theories with the following effective
Chern-Simons
levels

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{LHS:}\\
&(1):~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a+1&a+2&2\\
a+2&a+4&2\\
2&2&1\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a-3&a-2&-2\\
a-2&a&-2\\
-2&-2&0\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{1}{1-a}&\frac{a-2}{a-1}&-\frac{2}{a-1}\\
\frac{a-2}{a-1}&\frac{1}{1-a}&-\frac{2}{a-1}\\
-\frac{2}{a-1}&-\frac{2}{a-1}&-\frac{4}{a-1}\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a-4}{a}&\frac{2}{a}-1&-\frac{4}{a}\\
\frac{2}{a}-1&\frac{a-1}{a}&\frac{2}{a}\\
-\frac{4}{a}&\frac{2}{a}&-\frac{4}{a}\\
\end{array}\right],\\
&(2):~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a}{a+1}&-\frac{a+2}{a+1}&-\frac{2}{a+1}\\
-\frac{a+2}{a+1}&\frac{a}{a+1}&-\frac{2}{a+1}\\
-\frac{2}{a+1}&-\frac{2}{a+1}&\frac{a-3}{a+1}\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\frac{4}{a}&\frac{a+2}{a}&-\frac{4}{a}\\
\frac{a+2}{a}&-\frac{1}{a}&\frac{2}{a}\\
-\frac{4}{a}&\frac{2}{a}&\frac{a-4}{a}\\
\end{array}\right],\\
&(3):~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\frac{4}{a-4}&\frac{a-2}{a-4}&\frac{4}{a-4}\\
\frac{a-2}{a-4}&\frac{1}{4-a}&-\frac{2}{a-4}\\
\frac{4}{a-4}&-\frac{2}{a-4}&-\frac{4}{a-4}\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-\frac{1}{a+3}&\frac{a+2}{a+3}&\frac{2}{a+3}\\
\frac{a+2}{a+3}&-\frac{1}{a+3}&\frac{2}{a+3}\\
\frac{2}{a+3}&\frac{2}{a+3}&\frac{a-1}{a+3}\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a}{a+4}&-\frac{a+2}{a+4}&\frac{4}{a+4}\\
-\frac{a+2}{a+4}&\frac{a+3}{a+4}&-\frac{2}{a+4}\\
\frac{4}{a+4}&-\frac{2}{a+4}&\frac{a}{a+4}\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a-4}{a-3}&\frac{2-a}{a-3}&\frac{2}{a-3}\\
\frac{2-a}{a-3}&\frac{a-4}{a-3}&\frac{2}{a-3}\\
\frac{2}{a-3}&\frac{2}{a-3}&-\frac{4}{a-3}\\
\end{array}\right],\\
&(4):~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a+4}{4}&\frac{a+2}{4}&-1\\
\frac{a+2}{4}&\frac{a+4}{4}&-1\\
-1&-1&1\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a}{4}&\frac{a-2}{4}&1\\
\frac{a-2}{4}&\frac{a}{4}&1\\
1&1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(182)

We can now pick up integral matrices from RHS and LHS above and form dual pairs; they are in fact identified as linking (
\mathbf{B}
) and unlinking (
\mathbf{A}
) respectively:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS }~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\text{LHS}:\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}a+1&1\\
1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\quad&\longleftrightarrow\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a+1&a+2&2\\
a+2&a+4&2\\
2&2&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}a&-1\\
-1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\quad&\longleftrightarrow\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}a-3&a-2&-2\\
a-2&a&-2\\
-2&-2&0\\
\end{array}\right]\end{split}

(183)

In addition to these dual pairs, one can also introduce the following parameters to the last three matrices of the two-node graph (RHS) in (
181
) to make them formally integer

\displaystyle a=-1+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad a=\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad a=1+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad\forall\,n\in\mathbf{Z}\,.

(184)

For
n=\pm 1
we get
a=-2,0,\pm 1
, which are special since these values are allowed for the two-node graph in (
180
), which describes the theory
U(1)_{a}+1\mathbf{F}+1\mathbf{AF}
. We can also use another parameter
r=-n
for these three matrices, which yields

\displaystyle r=\frac{a}{1+a}\,,~~-\frac{1}{a}\,,~~\frac{1}{1-a}~~\in~\mathbf{Z}.

(185)

We rewrite the above
2\times 2
matrices in terms of
r
and find they are dual to
3\times 3
matrices in the first
(1)
and second
(2)
line of LHS in (
182
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS }~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\text{LHS}:\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&r-1\\
r-1&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}r&r-2&2(r-1)\\
r-2&r&2(r-1)\\
2(r-1)&2(r-1)&4r-3\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r+1&-r\\
-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}r+1&-2r-1&-2r\\
-2r-1&4r+1&4r\\
-2r&4r&4r\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}r&1-2r&-2r\\
1-2r&4r&4r\\
-2r&4r&4r+1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}r&r\\
r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}r&r+1&2r\\
r+1&r&2r\\
2r&2r&4r\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\end{split}

(186)

These dual pairs are identified respectively as
\textbf{A},\textbf{E},\textbf{B}
operations.

Furthermore, preferred parameters for the third line
(3)
of LHS are respectively:

\displaystyle a=4+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad a=-3+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad a=-4+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad a=3+\frac{1}{n}\,,\quad\forall\,n\in\mathbf{Z}\,.

(187)

Rewriting the matrices in the third line in terms of these parameters does not give rise to literally integer matrices, however they are in the same
ST
-orbit of
\textbf{A},\textbf{B},\textbf{E}
triangles (we do not show these matrices here).
For
n=\pm 1
in (
187
), we get special values
a=\pm 2,\pm 3,\pm 4,\pm 5
, which are allowed for the theory
U(1)_{a}+1\mathbf{F}+1\mathbf{AF}
in (
180
).
There is only one overlapping value
a=-2
for both (
184
) and (
187
), so
a=-2
should be a special value that leads to the maximal number of dual graphs with matrices:

\displaystyle\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-1&1\\
1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}-2&-1\\
-1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\\
\end{array}\right]~\longleftrightarrow~\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-1&0&1\\
0&-1&2\\
1&2&-3\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}2&0&2\\
0&2&2\\
2&2&5\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-1&0&2\\
0&2&2\\
2&2&1\\
\end{array}\right],\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}-5&-4&-2\\
-4&-2&-2\\
-2&-2&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,.

Note that matrices in the fourth line
(4)
of LHS of (
182
) cannot be integral for any
a
, so they are meaningless in this context.

External nodes

The 2-2 move and the 2-3 move that we found by partial gauging can be also coupled to external nodes. Let us show that in the presence of external nodes, the 2-2 move arising from partial gauging is a special case of the results found by full gauging discussed in section
4.2
. For the 2-2 move, adding an external node
U(1)_{c_{i}}
to (
153
) leads to

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/higgsinggeneric}\end{split}

(206)

Similarly to the full gauging, we do not need to apply a Kirby move to the external node
U(1)_{c_{i}}
. After scanning the orbit of theories given by
ST
-moves of the right graph in (
206
), we find many matrices with submatrices of the form (
4.3
). One subset of this orbit of theories is given by the following matrices

\displaystyle\text{RHS:}\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}{c_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}&0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&b+1&-1\\
0&-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}{c_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}&0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&b&1\\
0&1&0\\
\end{array}\right]

which are linear graphs given by
ST
-moving the
\mathbf{AF}
using the triality (
75
). Moreover, since these four matrices are in the same orbit of
ST
-moves, it seems that
ST
-moves change the sign of
b_{i}
. We will discuss in the next section that this implies the possibility of Higgsing a pair of
1\mathbf{F}+1\mathbf{AF}
.

The graph on LHS of (
206
) leads to four plumbing graphs in the orbit of
ST
-moves:

\displaystyle\text{LHS:}\quad\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}c_{i}&\pm b_{i}&\pm b_{i}\\
\hline\cr\pm b_{i}&b+1&b\\
\pm b_{i}&b&b\\
\end{array}\right]

which also are triangle graphs. The RHS relates to LHS through unlinking and linking as well, up to an element coming from the decoupled adjoint chiral multiplet on the LHS. If this decoupled matter node is added, the LHS is encoded in
3\times 3
matrices representing triangle graphs:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}{c_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}&0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&b+1&-1\\
0&-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]&~~\xlongrightarrow{~\text{linking}~}~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c|cc}{c_{i}}&~0&\pm{b_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}\\
\hline\cr 0&1&0&0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&0&b+1&b\\
\pm{b_{i}}&0&b&b\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{c|ccc}{c_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}&~0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&b&1\\
0&1&0\\
\end{array}\right]&~~\xlongrightarrow{\text{unlinking}}~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c|cc}{c_{i}}&~0&\pm{b_{i}}&\pm{b_{i}}\\
\hline\cr 0&0&0&0\\
\hline\cr\pm{b_{i}}&0&b+1&b\\
\pm{b_{i}}&0&b&b\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(216)

These are therefore dual pairs between linear graphs and triangle graphs, generalizing the dual pairs (
174
) by coupling to an external node. One can also go from the right to the left of (
216
), which would be identified with exotic operations; however, the element
1
for the decoupled chiral multiplet may be replaced equivalently by
0
to satisfy the relations for exotic triangles in (
132
).

Let us discuss the generalization of the dual pair (
177
) to include external nodes. In this case, both graphs in (
206
) can be transformed into linear or triangle graphs through
ST
- and Kirby moves:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\includegraphics[width]{Figures/higgsingRHS}\end{split}

(217)

The corresponding dual pairs are represented by matrices, which in appropriate parametrization take form

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS}~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\text{LHS}\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}{C_{i}}&B_{i}&-{B_{i}}\\
\hline\cr{B_{i}}&r&1-r\\
-{B_{i}}&1-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&B_{i}&-B_{i}\\
\hline\cr B_{i}&r&-r\\
-B_{i}&-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&\pm B_{i}&\pm B_{i}\\
\hline\cr\pm B_{i}&r+1&r\\
\pm B_{i}&r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&\pm B_{i}&0\\
\hline\cr\pm B_{i}&r+1&-1\\
0&-1&1\\
\end{array}\right]\,,~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&\pm B_{i}&0\\
\hline\cr\pm B_{i}&r&1\\
0&1&0\\
\end{array}\right]\,,\\
\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&B_{i}&-B_{i}\\
\hline\cr B_{i}&r&-r\\
-B_{i}&-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]~~&\longleftrightarrow~~\left[\begin{array}[]{c|cc}C_{i}&B_{i}&-B_{i}\\
\hline\cr B_{i}&r&1-r\\
-B_{i}&1-r&r\\
\end{array}\right]\,.\end{split}

(218)

Note that the first and the third line are the same, and represent a dual pair of triangle graphs. The second line represents a duality between a triangle and linear graphs.

Analogously one can discuss 2-3 move given by partial gauging the other flavor symmetry
U(1)_{y}
. In this case the results are a generalization of (
183
) and (
186
), as an interested reader can check.

4.4
Higgsing

Finally, we discuss Higgsing of matter fields, which happens when mass parameters are tuned to some specific values, so that the multiplet
\mathbf{F}
cancels with
\mathbf{AF}

\text{Higgsing:}\quad 1\mathbf{F}+1\mathbf{AF}\rightarrow 1

In this case we can delete from the graph these two matter nodes and any lines connecting to them

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hbox to87.01pt{\vbox to32.19pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 16.67297pt\lower-28.85275pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{46.8117pt}{-17.4486pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
